<a id="task-cloud-01"></a>

### CLOUD.01 — Ingress and host pipeline

**Outcome.** Worker /api routing plus the C# AOT gRPC-Web/auth/current-owner pipeline runs behind the Worker in the real Container image; no buffered stream, no direct public Container port.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Owning repository | Cloud (`C:\MyFile\Projects\ArcForges\Cloud`); integration owner: Cloud integration owner, the holder of `roles/integration-cloud` |
| Claim, branch and ledger | `claims/cloud-01` and ledger record `ledger/tasks/cloud-01.md` in the Plan repository; task branch `task/cloud-01` ([DLV-26](../README.md#rule-dlv-26)) |
| Kind / size | service / M · early risk proof |
| Obligations | [WP-21.00](../../work-packages/21-cloud-host-and-persistence.md#rule-wp-21.00) — all work except the parts mapped to CLOUD.39 |
| Provides | cloud-ingress-pipeline |
| Start prerequisites | **contract** [CON.91](contracts.md#task-con-91) — published ArcForges.Contracts.PublicApi generated gRPC-Web service stubs to register the pipeline against. *Why:* the pipeline has nothing to route without at least one generated service surface |
| Entry condition | [ADOPT.07.cloud](adoption.md#task-adopt-07-cloud) — the adoption slice for this repository and lane is complete ([DLV-22](../README.md#rule-dlv-22)) |
| Completion prerequisites | none |
| Unblocks | [CLOUD.02](#task-cloud-02), [CLOUD.05](#task-cloud-05), [CLOUD.08](#task-cloud-08), [CLOUD.09](#task-cloud-09), [CLOUD.10](#task-cloud-10), [CLOUD.19](#task-cloud-19), [CLOUD.39](#task-cloud-39), [CLOUD.42](#task-cloud-42), [CLOUD.69](#task-cloud-69), [CLOUD.70](#task-cloud-70), [CLOUD.84](#task-cloud-84), [HAR.00](harness.md#task-har-00), [HAR.40](harness.md#task-har-40), [PLT.48](platform.md#task-plt-48) |
| Write scope | `Cloud:worker/index.ts`<br>`Cloud:worker/router.ts`<br>`Cloud:worker/ingress/** (new: the generalized /api route table, credential and CSRF edge checks and the frame-guarded streaming pass-through)`<br>`Cloud:worker/foundation/entry.ts, Cloud:worker/foundation/container-env.ts and Cloud:worker/foundation/types.ts (only to hand the ingress route table the proof-only probe route and to forward the allowed origin; no change to a proof route, queue consumer or private handler)`<br>`Cloud:wrangler.json`<br>`Cloud:Dockerfile`<br>`Cloud:.dockerignore (only admit the new nested C# source folder of the existing host project by the same append-only explicit directory rule and *.cs rule used for its other source folders; every other ignore rule is preserved)`<br>`Cloud:src/ArcForges.Cloud/** (the existing Native AOT host project, which is the real layout of the planned src/ArcForges.Cloud.Host/**; the adoption rules forbid renaming it or adding a parallel project)`<br>`Cloud:tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/**`<br>`Cloud:tests/worker/**`<br>`Cloud:eng/verification/** (the local cross-process and explicit opt-in live scenarios of the pipeline)`<br>`Cloud:package.json (the explicit test file list and the pipeline scenario scripts only; no dependency or version change)`<br>`Cloud:eng/policy/dependency-policy.json and Cloud:eng/policy/dependency-reviews/cloud-01-*.json (new immutable successor chained from the then-active receipt, only because the changed bundle, manifest and source inputs are hash-bound; no coordinate, integrity value or closure entry changes)`<br>`Cloud:eng/provenance/** (immutable successor release profile, Worker bundle and runtime-notice records only where an existing record binds an input this task changes, the first-party inventory files.json and the deterministic NOTICE.txt)`<br>`Cloud:docs/cloud-ingress.md, Cloud:docs/deployment.md and Cloud:AGENTS.md (factual description of the ingress pipeline; the AGENTS.md Hello bullet is narrowed to what remains true)` |
| Shared resources | [RES-cloud-deployment](../shared-resources.md#res-cloud-deployment) (append), [RES-cloud-host-composition](../shared-resources.md#res-cloud-host-composition) (append) |
| Validation | offline unit tests for routing/validation logic; deployed-environment request/stream/cancel/CSRF/trailer path checks are opt-in local runtime evidence per docs/validation-policy.md, not hosted CI |
| Completion evidence | source commit, Worker/Container image hash, deployed request/stream/cancel/CSRF/trailer scenario results, confirmation no buffered stream or direct public Container port exists |
| Baseline (unreviewed unless accepted) | not-started Observed scaffold, unreviewed: Cloud@ce0a32a has only a Hello World Worker (worker/router.ts handles exactly /api/arcforges.hello.v1.HelloService/SayHello + /api/healthz) and a Hello World C# host (src/ArcForges.Cloud: BuildIdentity/HealthStatus/HelloEndpoint/Program). No real routing, auth, or module dispatch exists. |
| Notes | Foundation task. The Hello World router.ts already proves the deadline/cancel/body-limit/trailer mechanics at small scale (see worker/router.ts); WP21.00 generalizes this to real dispatch. Early risk: every later public call depends on this boundary being correct. Planning repair 2026-10-08 ([DLV-34](../README.md#rule-dlv-34); [P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021); adjudication 5): the worker/index.ts and worker/router.ts transport adapter stays thin. Its TypeScript credential, CSRF and Origin edge checks are kept only under the [P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021) edge-guard rule: C# remains authoritative and enforces the same rule, their parameters and allowlists are generated from the C# source, and the Worker only refuses early (a cost control) and never grants. No user decision is needed for them. The remaining business decisions move to C# under CLOUD.84. Outcome, validation and evidence above are recorded history and are unchanged. |

<a id="task-cloud-02"></a>

### CLOUD.02 — Nineteen module boundaries and D1 named-plan bridge

**Outcome.** The 19 module projects exist as boundaries and the D1 named-plan bridge mechanism works: C# decides business logic and asks the Worker to execute one exact named/versioned plan; the Worker executes only approved SQL, never ad hoc queries.

<a id="task-cloud-05"></a>

### CLOUD.05 — Finite durable jobs (Cron, Queue and DO-alarm wake into C# endpoints)

**Outcome.** Perpetual hosted loops are replaced by Cron-, Queue- and Durable-Object-alarm-woken C# endpoints bounded to <=100 items/20s per job with checkpoint/receipt/lease-then-yield semantics. No Cloudflare Workflow holds job state or wakes a job in this task; each wake is a stateless forward into a C# endpoint, and C# makes every step decision. A later Workflow wake adapter needs its own reviewed record ([P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021) item 5).

<a id="task-cloud-06"></a>

### CLOUD.06 — Shared atomic family guarded-batch engine

**Outcome.** A reusable D1 guarded-batch engine exists that enforces the fixed [SU-04](../../../architecture/04-desktop-application-architecture.md#rule-su-04) module lock order (Config->Identity->Workspace->Device->Entitlement->Commerce->Policy->Agent->Chat->Scope->Task->Search->PackageCatalog->Notification->Resource->Sync->Audit) and provides authorization/revision/policy/balance/lease guard primitives that any shared-transaction family can compose: a closed registry of families with their participant lists, a family plan grammar whose generator expands the guard primitives against the physical manifest and refuses a plan that is out of order, names a table of another participant's module or lets a module write without its own guard, the platform_command_guard table that makes a false guard roll the whole batch back, and a C# unit of work and executor that seal one family plan call, supply the command identity to every guard, and reread and recalculate with bounded jitter only under the original command. The module tasks append their own family and plans (RES-shared-transaction-families); this task ships the registry empty and no family plan.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Owning repository | Cloud (`C:\MyFile\Projects\ArcForges\Cloud`); integration owner: Cloud integration owner, the holder of `roles/integration-cloud` |
| Claim, branch and ledger | `claims/cloud-06` and ledger record `ledger/tasks/cloud-06.md` in the Plan repository; task branch `task/cloud-06` ([DLV-26](../README.md#rule-dlv-26)) |
| Kind / size | service / M |
| Obligations | [WP-21.05](../../work-packages/21-cloud-host-and-persistence.md#rule-wp-21.05) — generic guarded-batch engine and fixed [SU-04](../../../architecture/04-desktop-application-architecture.md#rule-su-04) module lock-order enforcement only; each module's own family participant list is a separate obligation carried by that module's own task (see coverage) |
| Provides | shared-atomic-family-engine |
| Start prerequisites | **artifact** [CLOUD.02](#task-cloud-02) — the D1 named-plan bridge, since a guarded batch is executed as one named plan. *Why:* the family engine is built on top of the plan-execution mechanism, not a separate execution path<br>**artifact** [CLOUD.03](#task-cloud-03) — the physical manifest and migration runner: the guard primitives are expanded against the physical columns and the guard table is one more manifest table and expand migration. *Why:* a guard names real table and column types, and the platform_command_guard table joins the locked migration sequence |

Decisions recorded in this repair (raised to the Architecture Owner, [D-001](../../../decisions/phase-1-foundation-decisions.md#rule-d-001), in the pull request): (1) a realm is deployment configuration, not a table (one D1 authority per realm, model 00 section 6.1.1; model 01 declares `realm_id` columns and no realm table), so the core model carries a realm value and every plan and rule scopes by it; (2) no task in the graph owns the Workspace module's tables, so the single-owner workspace provisioning is carried here, as the family statements of module `workspace` in the enrollment family and the minimal workspace model of the Identity core; (3) the enrollment row of the shared units of work (data model 00 section 6.1.1, rule su-01 in docs/architecture/data-model/00-data-model-overview.md: 'Authentication/enrollment completion and default workspace provisioning') lists Device, Entitlement and Notification, which belong to CLOUD.12/13/19 and the grant tasks, so the registered `account-enrollment` family declares Identity and Workspace as required participants and Device, Entitlement and Notification as conditional on the completion that creates a session, applies configured initial grants or notifies, and the later tasks append their own plans of the same family; (4) the module cannot reference the plan bridge, so the typed bridge from the module's statements to a plan call is the Abstractions-level port that COM.16 delivers; binding the Identity store port (IIdentityStore) to it, the production identifier source and the receipt reused-identifier mapping are owned by CLOUD.72 (planning repair 2026-10-05, after this task completed without a production store; CLOUD.12 starts after CLOUD.72), and this task proves the plans against the real migrations and the shared vectors instead; (5) the plans declare the commit tail that CLOUD.04 delivered, so a CLOUD.04 artifact start edge is added (CLOUD.04 is complete, so it changes no ordering). No membership, role, invitation or seat concept is added; the structural tests that forbid it are part of this task. Planning repair 2026-10-08 ([DLV-34](../README.md#rule-dlv-34); [P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021)): the identity plans under storage/plans/identity move to C# under CLOUD.84; the model, rules, service and plans are unchanged. Outcome, validation and evidence above are recorded history and are unchanged. |

<a id="task-cloud-12"></a>

### CLOUD.12 — Native and browser authentication with Postmark/SES mail adapters (live delivery evidence blocked-external)

**Outcome.** The contracts 07 native authorize/token PKCE ceremony, the provider callback and a minimal server-rendered authorization page work in the C# Native AOT host over the C# Identity module. The official realm offers passkey and email one-time code only. A self-host realm offers its operator-configured local password, passkey and OIDC providers, and an enterprise identity provider is configured as an OIDC-kind provider ([ID-25](../../../requirements/02-identity-account-and-workspace.md#rule-id-25), security 08 self-host authentication, brief S6); SAML is not specified and is not built. The official-realm password and the official social or enterprise sign-in methods are out of scope, not completed ([ID-21](../../../requirements/02-identity-account-and-workspace.md#rule-id-21), [ID-24](../../../requirements/02-identity-account-and-workspace.md#rule-id-24), [ID-26](../../../requirements/02-identity-account-and-workspace.md#rule-id-26); [P2-026](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-026) S19(c)). Authentication and enrollment completion commit through the account-enrollment family with the Device participant of CLOUD.13 and the Notification security-notification statement, so a new installation's first session, its lowest-trust installation and the notification commit together; session issuance and native refresh (rotation with spent-token reuse detection) run over the CLOUD.13 session store. The Identity pre-auth flow service (identity_browser_auth_flow) has this one owner, and CLOUD.19 reuses it. The Identity grant services (the self-host one-use enrollment grant and the administrator recovery grant that CLOUD.17 consumes) are implemented here; their operator-surface invocation is an open OPS-lane gap. The generated IdentityService authentication methods and this task's session and mail-callback plain paths are declared in the Cloud host through the CLOUD.21 route-declaration mechanism. Real Postmark-primary/SES-secondary delivery and outcome adapters are delivered as complete production adapters in the C# Notification module, which also owns the durable notification_notification row statements and plans. They reach the providers only through the Container's thin external-egress handler, and test doubles (SUB-postmark-ses-test-recordings) are used only inside tests at the provider boundary. Live provider delivery and recovery evidence is a blocked-external input under [P2-025](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-025) and section 0e that gates completion, not delivery. TypeScript holds only thin forwarding and egress adapters whose tables are generated from C# ([P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021) item 1).

<a id="task-cloud-13"></a>

### CLOUD.13 — Device, installation, instance and session (four distinct concepts)

**Outcome.** Device, installation, instance and session are four distinct concepts with four lifecycles. A device (Devices module, device_device) has a server-issued random identifier that is never a hardware fingerprint and survives ordinary hardware change. An installation (device_installation) is one product on one device with its own key and key version. An instance is a running process of an installation, distinguished by an instance epoch, and has no D1 table (data model 01: presence is the ApplicationPresence projection of DEV.01). A session (Identity module, identity_session) belongs to one device and installation and has the native-bearer or browser-cookie credential shape; this task owns the session record and its store. Device revocation is one device-revocation family commit (Identity + Device + Notification, data model 00 section 6.1.1): the device is denied, and its sessions and push registrations are revoked together. The Device participant of account-enrollment creates the first installation of a new device. The native session-verification port backs the host binding of the bearer verifier (IBearerTokenVerifier), and an additive cross-module contribution seam on the CLOUD.72 family port, added after CLOUD.72 delivers, lets each participant module contribute only its own statements to a multi-module family. Step-up on device revocation and revoke-all-sessions is wired by CLOUD.15.

### CLOUD.14 — Device trust and remote gating

**Outcome.** Trust levels per device exist with remote access defaulting to off; raising trust requires an explicit act with step-up; remote capability is derived from trust, never from mere session possession.

<a id="task-cloud-15"></a>

### CLOUD.15 — Step-up challenges for sensitive operations

**Outcome.** Step-up challenges exist for the enumerated sensitive operations of requirements 02 section 6 whose published operation metadata sets stepUp. A challenge re-asserts a CLOUD.12 method (passkey, fresh email code, self-host password or OIDC). Its evidence binds actor, session, recovery generation, operation class and target hash, and is valid for at most five minutes and one sensitive action. An app unlock never substitutes. Step-up state is per session and per operation class, never a global elevated mode. The Identity and Device operations delivered before this task (passkey registration on an existing account, credential removal, password change, revoke-all-sessions and device revocation) demand the evidence here, and BeginStepUp and CompleteStepUp are registered as generated IdentityService methods. Trust elevation and remote enablement (CLOUD.14), high-privilege token creation (CLOUD.16), recovery-code generation and account deletion (CLOUD.17) and email change (CLOUD.19) demand it in their owners' tasks, which start on this task and whose completion closes this coverage. Workspace data deletion (workspace.requestDataDeletion) is wired by CLOUD.66. Connector authorisation is out of scope ([P2-026](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-026) S5).

<a id="task-cloud-17"></a>

### CLOUD.17 — Recovery, account states and deletion

**Outcome.** Recovery flows (email code, recovery code, registered passkey and, in a self-host realm, an audited administrator grant issued by the CLOUD.12 grant service) resist modelled abuse. Each completed recovery commits atomically through the account-security-change family: the proof is consumed, the recovery-code set is invalidated, sessions, PATs and pending native authorization codes are revoked, the credential changes and the 'recovery used' security notification is queued. Recovery-code generation follows step-up. Account states (active, restricted, suspended, pending-deletion, deleted) have defined capability. Deletion follows step-up and a preview. It has a grace period that can be cancelled only through a fresh cancelDeletion session and an explicit scope of what is and isn't deleted. It revokes ordinary sessions, devices, API tokens and remote access at request time through the device-revocation family, and it never touches local data. At grace expiry a finite job purges the account's Identity-, Device- and Notification-owned rows, and the task publishes a deletion-participant port through which each content module purges its own rows; the content modules' purge is assigned to CLOUD.20 by record.

<a id="task-cloud-18"></a>

### CLOUD.18 — Independent native session integration (Platform client primitives)

**Outcome.** System browser, per-product redirects, secure storage and installation-bound tokens are integrated into a new C# DesktopPlatform client primitive, ArcForges.Security.Sessions, over the PLT.40 secret broker. Each desktop client owns its own session, with no token sharing and no device SSO; tokens and pending flows live only in the OS secret store, and only the per-realm session metadata of data model 02 (recovery generation and installation identity, never a token) is kept in the assistant store. The primitive targets Windows and Linux only ([P2-023](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-023): no macOS) and fails closed, with no plaintext fallback, on a platform without an OS secret-store adapter; on Linux this is 'blocked on a Linux secret-store adapter (PLT.40 follow-up), not proven'. Android native sessions belong to the MAUI app (AND.06, AND.08) and never use this AGPL package ([P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021) item 3).

<a id="task-cloud-19"></a>

### CLOUD.19 — Browser cookie-session adapter and full account-surface closure

**Outcome.** The same-origin browser adapter runs in the Native AOT host (src/ArcForges.Cloud, Session/Browser). Over the CLOUD.13 session record and store and the CLOUD.12 browser pre-auth flow it provides random hashed session, preauth and CSRF values, exact Origin checks, idle and absolute expiry, a lowest-trust browser installation and a one-use auth flow, with explicit cookie parsing and writing (no ASP.NET Data Protection or cookie-authentication middleware). The /session/v1 route catalogue of the published Contracts browser schema is mapped to the existing owner application ports, declared through the CLOUD.21 route-declaration mechanism and forwarded by the Cloud Worker's generated, thin, host-agnostic forwarding; it is deployed and exercised on the proof origin, where the Blazor Account profile is served. A restricted cancel-deletion session admits only getAccountDeletion, cancelAccountDeletion and logout. The full typed account surface is wired through the same owner ports and registered through the CLOUD.21 endpoint registry: profile and email, recovery-code set, scoped PAT, credential rename, session listing, the four sign-out scopes, per-installation browser authorization, remote capability policy and restricted deletion-cancel reauthentication. The two-products-under-one-OS-user isolation check is out of scope under [P2-026](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-026) item 20 (a single in-scope product), recorded out of scope, not completed.

<a id="task-cloud-21"></a>

### CLOUD.21 — Public endpoint mapping and validation

**Outcome.** Generated proto service methods of the pinned ArcForges.Contracts.PublicApi candidate are registered in the Native AOT host (src/ArcForges.Cloud, PublicApi/Endpoints). The deny-by-default policy table is built from the published Contracts operation metadata (CON.41), and the generated ContractShapeValidation of the same candidate validates request and reply exactly before any owner code runs. Binary gRPC-Web unary calls and declared server streams go through the same owner ports. Owner mutations and the Sync allowlist are mapped exactly. Every generated public method is listed as registered with its owner or as unregistered with its closing task. This task owns the module route-declaration mechanism: host modules and module boundaries declare their RpcPolicies and plain paths, the C# generator reads them with their TransportBudgets rows fail-closed, and the Worker route table and its thin forwarding are generated from these declarations, so owner tasks only append declarations. The registered methods reach production through the existing gated main-push deployment job and the proof origin through one proof deploy. No ad-hoc REST business API exists.

<a id="task-cloud-22"></a>

### CLOUD.22 — Typed protocol and error mapping

**Outcome.** The generated response outcome (a value or an ArcError, the ArcResult of [OC-04](../../../architecture/contracts/00-operation-catalogue.md#rule-oc-04)) and the gRPC-Web transport statuses and trailers are mapped exactly under registry 04 by one mapper in the host (src/ArcForges.Cloud, PublicApi/Errors). A domain refusal is gRPC OK with outcome.error carrying a code of the closed set, under the category, retry and effect rules that the Contracts validators define. A transport failure is a status without a fabricated ArcError, and it preserves whether dispatch may have happened. ProblemDetails is limited to the documented HTTP exceptions: a Contracts-declared error body where one exists, otherwise the status alone; no new HTTP-exception body shape is introduced.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Owning repository | Cloud (`C:\MyFile\Projects\ArcForges\Cloud`); integration owner: Cloud integration owner, the holder of `roles/integration-cloud` |
| Claim, branch and ledger | `claims/cloud-22` and ledger record `ledger/tasks/cloud-22.md` in the Plan repository; task branch `task/cloud-22` ([DLV-26](../README.md#rule-dlv-26)) |
| Kind / size | service / M |
| Obligations | [WP-23.01](../../work-packages/23-public-api-and-generated-clients.md#rule-wp-23.01) — full |
| Provides | typed-error-mapping |
| Start prerequisites | **artifact** [CLOUD.21](#task-cloud-21) — the endpoint registration to attach error mapping to. *Why:* error mapping wraps the handlers CLOUD.21 registers |
| Entry condition | [ADOPT.07.cloud](adoption.md#task-adopt-07-cloud) — the adoption slice for this repository and lane is complete ([DLV-22](../README.md#rule-dlv-22)) |
| Completion prerequisites | none |
| Unblocks | [CLOUD.19](#task-cloud-19), [CLOUD.23](#task-cloud-23), [CLOUD.26](#task-cloud-26), [CLOUD.28](#task-cloud-28), [CLOUD.29](#task-cloud-29), [CLOUD.64](#task-cloud-64), [CLOUD.66](#task-cloud-66), [PRF.05](runtime-proofs.md#task-prf-05), [PRF.08](runtime-proofs.md#task-prf-08) — out of scope, [PRF.11](runtime-proofs.md#task-prf-11), [PRF.12](runtime-proofs.md#task-prf-12) |
| Write scope | `Cloud:src/ArcForges.Cloud/PublicApi/Errors/** (new folder of the existing host project). It holds three things. The first is the one mapper from typed owner-port results to the generated response outcome: a value, or an ArcError with a code from the closed registry 04 set, its category, messageKey, retry advice, effect certainty, correlation id and typed ErrorDetails. The second is the exception-to-transport-status table (INVALID_ARGUMENT, UNAUTHENTICATED, PERMISSION_DENIED, RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, UNAVAILABLE, DEADLINE_EXCEEDED, CANCELLED), preserving whether dispatch may have happened. The third is the HTTP-exception error writer that CLOUD.19's browser-session routes call: it writes the Contracts-declared error body where one exists (the OIDC callback failures) and elsewhere only the status code with Cache-Control no-store and no body; no new body shape is introduced, and the proof's ad hoc error JSON is not carried into production. A transport failure never fabricates an ArcError, and a domain refusal is never a transport status.`<br>`Cloud:src/ArcForges.Cloud/PublicApi/Endpoints/** (only the outcome call sites of methods registered at the time of the change, routed through the mapper, as CLOUD.23 does for list operations; an endpoint owner that registers a method after this task merges routes its outcomes through the mapper itself; CLOUD.21's registry and coverage are unchanged)`<br>`Cloud:src/ArcForges.Cloud/Ingress/GrpcWebFraming.cs, Cloud:src/ArcForges.Cloud/Ingress/IngressPipeline.cs and Cloud:src/ArcForges.Cloud/Ingress/Correlation.cs (only where the pipeline refusals and the CorrelationReplies ArcError builder must use the same mapping; existing statuses and keys change only where registry 04 requires it, and each such change keeps its test; RES-cloud-host-composition, shared with CLOUD.16's PAT admission lines in IngressPipeline.cs)`<br>`Cloud:.dockerignore (only the append-only admission of the new source folder)`<br>`Cloud:tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/PublicApi/Errors/** (new C# tests)`<br>`Cloud:eng/verification/** (only an explicit opt-in live scenario of deadline and cancel-after-dispatch through the proof Worker; never CI)`<br>`Cloud:eng/policy/dependency-policy.json and Cloud:eng/policy/dependency-reviews/cloud-22-*.json (new immutable successor, only if hash-bound inputs change)`<br>`Cloud:eng/provenance/** (immutable successor release profile, Worker bundle and runtime-notice records, only where an existing record binds an input this task changes; files.json and the deterministic NOTICE.txt)`<br>`Cloud:docs/cloud-ingress.md (factual: the error and status mapping section) and Cloud:AGENTS.md (only a factual pointer)` |

<a id="task-cloud-23"></a>

### CLOUD.23 — Typed queries and revision preconditions

**Outcome.** In the host's public endpoint layer (src/ArcForges.Cloud, PublicApi/Queries), opaque scope-bound PageRequest cursors work: each is signed with a key derived per realm from a configured Container secret (never in wrangler.json; documented rotation) and bound to scope, recoveryGeneration, operation, filter, the dataset token and the sort key, and is refused when forged, altered or foreign. Registered typed filters and RequestMeta expected-owner-revision preconditions work. A stale revision is refused as conflict.revision_mismatch through the one error mapper. No ETag or If-Match is used for business RPC, and no plan is written into another owner's plan directory.

<a id="task-cloud-29"></a>

### CLOUD.29 — Stream connection and authentication (EventService.Watch/ExecutionService.WatchOutput shells)

**Outcome.** Public server-streaming shells for EventService.Watch and ExecutionService.WatchOutput (arcforges.events.v1, served from the generated ArcForges.Contracts.Events service bases, one implementation class per service) run in the C# Native AOT host behind the Worker's frame-guarded pass-through. Every frame is a generated StreamFrame whose ResponseMeta carries the call's correlation identity through the CLOUD.69 seam. The current session and scope are authorized before the stream opens and re-authorized at least every 15 seconds by the same session-validity and current-owner check the ingress applies (CSRF and Origin stay admission-time checks); a revoke, expiry, epoch or recovery-generation change ends the stream with a typed terminal frame and status trailer and no further body. Every stream ends with exactly one gRPC status trailer, and a client cancel releases the server handler. WatchOutput's execution-output content comes from the Task-module seam HAR.03 fills (annex 10: WP24 substrate, WP52 content); this task emits no execution output of its own. Real C# (the generated client over Grpc.Net.Client.Web), browser (Blazor WebAssembly) and MAUI Android binary streams work against the deployed proof ingress with trailers, cancel and expiry. Each stream service sets its own gRPC message-size options, so stream frames are not held to the Hello limits. The browser uses binary server streaming through the .NET 10 browser streaming HttpClient; if the Blazor proof (PRF.11) records that binary server streaming does not work in the browser, the grpc-web-text variant is admitted on server-stream routes only, as recorded in the wire registry. No client or bidirectional stream and no WebSocket path; the private CloudInternal RunStreamService.Run is never reachable from the public listener, and StartTransientTurn, AcknowledgeOutput and PurgeTransient receive no policy from this task and stay refused. The Worker makes no decision: the route rows and stream budgets are generated from C# declarations.

<a id="task-cloud-31"></a>

### CLOUD.31 — Cursor and gap handling (DO projection backed by D1 outbox)

**Outcome.** Sequence, hash and offset cursors and snapshot high-water recovery work per annex 10 section 5, decided in C#: a missing or expired cursor answers a reset with snapshotRequired and a scoped high-water cursor, a duplicate identical frame is harmless, conflicting content at one position fails integrity, a gap is never reported as contiguous, and revisions replay idempotently. The EventFeed Durable Object is a thin TypeScript projection backed by the D1 outbox and never a second business authority: it holds bounded hint rows by authorized scope and sequence within the generated limits, as opaque bytes that C# encodes as generated arcforges.events.v1 messages (no new proto), and makes no cursor, gap or authorization decision. Which committed outbox event becomes which public hint is decided by a closed C# event-hint registry owned by this task: each emitting module supplies its pure payload projection, and an event type without a registered projection is refused. A deleted or truncated projection is detected against the D1 outbox watermark and answered from the committed outbox or by a reset, never by inventing contiguity; refilling the projection is the CLOUD.33 publisher.

<a id="task-cloud-34"></a>

### CLOUD.34 — Bounded stream lifecycle

**Outcome.** The server enforces the annex 10 section 5 lifecycle: a stream closes normally after five minutes with one OK trailer, a heartbeat frame goes out every 15 seconds so a client can detect 45 seconds of silence, at most 256 KiB is queued per slow reader and an overflow resets or terminates rather than growing or dropping bytes while reporting contiguous output, and a client close at any point (an Android background close included) releases the server stream, after which a later Poll or ReadOutput (CLOUD.32) returns current state. No unbounded memory and no WebSocket hibernation-cost assumption. The client halves of [WP-24.05](../../work-packages/24-realtime-and-reliable-events.md#rule-wp-24.05) move by this repair to named recipients ([P2-026](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-026) item 20): reconnect after 45 seconds of silence and exponential full jitter from 0.5 to 30 seconds that resets after 30 seconds of stable connectivity to CLOUD.35 (the C# Cloud.Client stream adapter) and AND.04 (the Mobile stream adapter), and the Android background close with its later refetch to AND.04; this task proves the server side of them.

<a id="task-cloud-69"></a>

### CLOUD.69 — Correlation acceptance and propagation across ingress, response meta and queue wake

**Outcome.** The deployed Cloud ingress and host carry one correlation identity per call ([CR-01](../../../architecture/09-ai-and-agent-runtime-architecture.md#rule-cr-01), [CR-03](../../../architecture/09-ai-and-agent-runtime-architecture.md#rule-cr-03), [CR-06](../../../architecture/13-observability-and-operations.md#rule-cr-06), [HP-06](../../../architecture/05-cloud-architecture.md#rule-hp-06)): a RequestMeta.correlationId is read, validated as a canonical lowercase UUID and bound to the call context without ever influencing authorization or the current-owner decision, an absent value is created at the edge, a malformed value is refused with the registered validation.invalid_request code, and the identity is returned in ResponseMeta.correlationId and ArcError.correlationId of every reply the host builds. The Worker and host join it to one W3C traceparent per call, and the job wake message and the private job-slice call carry correlationId and causationId (the originating request or the previous wake event), so one synthetic call is joined across HTTP, Worker, host and queue-wake by the identifier alone. The task provides the one Cloud-side seam (a call-scoped correlation context for the host and a correlation envelope for Worker-originated queue messages) that later hop owners reuse instead of re-implementing propagation per module. It adds no realtime or provider behavior and no new wire field, header or contract meaning: a need for one is raised to the Contracts owner under [PA-02](../../implementation-sequence.md#rule-pa-02).

| Owning repository | Cloud (`C:\MyFile\Projects\ArcForges\Cloud`); integration owner: Cloud integration owner, the holder of `roles/integration-cloud` |

This task is that owner and is deliberately small: it uses only the fields already in wire registry 04 (RequestMeta tag 3, ResponseMeta tag 4, ArcError tag 6; the generated Contracts packages already carry them) and the existing private signed job-slice call. It does not add the realtime or provider hops: Event.correlationId is populated by the publication owners (CLOUD.33 and the stream owners) and a provider request identifier ([CR-05](../../../architecture/09-ai-and-agent-runtime-architecture.md#rule-cr-05)) by the provider owners (AIR.04), each of which starts from this task's seam and keeps its own acceptance. The C# origin that makes the connected trace is not a Cloud or DesktopPlatform delivery task: PLT.48's completion follow-up performs it as one local opt-in consumer run outside the repository (the PLT.53 precedent) using the published ArcForges.Observability package to create the typed origin and the published generated PublicApi client to send it to the isolated proof environment, under the RES-cloud-deployment lease, and records the connected trace. The write scope of CLOUD.01 is not reopened. Outcome, validation and evidence above are recorded history and are unchanged. |

<a id="task-cloud-70"></a>

### CLOUD.70 — Gated D1 migration deployment step and compatible-rollback flow

**Outcome.** Migrations reach a deployed D1 database only through one gated step of the deployment pipeline (Design D1 profile section 6: never from Container startup). The step runs the CLOUD.03 runner against the target environment's business database under the migrator lease and fence, applies the pending migrations in sequence, prints the receipts and compatibility record without any secret, applies a backfill, cutover or contract migration only when the release manifest names it for this deployment (contract only with the explicit consent flag and after its soak), and before promoting a Worker or image checks the compatible-rollback rule so that a build the read or write horizon excludes is never promoted. The step is exercised once against the proof environment under the RES-cloud-deployment lease and records its result; production receives the same step only through the existing gated main-push deployment job. It adds no schema, no runner behavior and no deployment secret to pull-request code.

<a id="task-cloud-71"></a>

### CLOUD.71 — Serve the built Web profiles from the proof origin

**Outcome.** The two production profile builds of PRF.08 are served, byte for byte, by the proof Worker (custom domain proof.arcforges.com, workers.dev and previews stay disabled) as static assets on separate paths of the same origin that serves /api, /session/v1 and the operator-signed /proof/v1 surface, which keep going to the Worker first. The bytes come from one immutable digest-named bundle that the Web main-push build publishes; the Cloud proof deployment job (manual proof=deploy) downloads that exact asset, verifies its digest against the value pinned in the deployment manifest and binds it as the Worker's assets, so nothing is rebuilt and no Web source is read. Responses carry the profile Content-Security-Policy that PRF.08 derives. Each profile is built with its own router base, /account/ and /chat/, so that the unchanged page code runs on its own path (a profile whose router base is / answers "Page not found" on any other path); the compiled JavaScript and stylesheet chunks stay byte-identical to the root builds, only the prerendered page moves to account/index.html and chat/index.html and carries its base, and the content-hashed assets/ directories of the two builds merge into one tree in which a name with two different contents refuses the bundle. Production configuration and the Web apex Custom Domain are untouched. The proof Container application gives its two named instances (hello, which serves /api, and foundation, which serves /session/v1 and the operator surface) the headroom to run at the same time, and a read-only observation in the manual proof dispatch shows the application's configured ceiling and its instances before and after the deployment. The result is exercised once with PRF.08's live script (apps/app/scripts/proof-run.ts) pointed at the served profiles under the RES-cloud-deployment lease and the run's result is recorded.

<a id="task-cloud-72"></a>

### CLOUD.72 — Identity production store, identifier source and enrollment family call over the plan-execution port

**Outcome.** The Identity module has a production store and identifier source, so its service can be resolved and served. IIdentityStore is implemented by one internal D1 store under the Identity project's own Persistence folder, written only against the Abstractions plan-execution port that COM.16 delivered (named identity plans with exact typed values; no SQL, no table name, no reference to the plan bridge); its commit variants call the named identity plans and the enrollment call executes the registered family `account-enrollment` (plan `families.account-enrollment.create-user`) through a generic family-execution port that this task adds to ArcForges.Cloud.Modules.Abstractions (outside the Identity folder, so every later family participant reuses it) and a Storage.D1 adapter that implements it over the existing shared-family engine, refusing a family or a statement contribution the calling module may not make. IIdentityIdSource is implemented with identifiers from the platform's cryptographic random generator in the canonical lower-case UUID form, so a user, credential or workspace identifier is unpredictable, and a collision is never an overwrite (the primary keys and the revision-zero guards refuse it as a whole-batch precondition and the commit retries with fresh identifiers). The receipt reused-identifier mapping that CLOUD.11 deferred is delivered: a repeated enrollment command with the same content is answered from its receipt as a replay (the service returns CreatedUser=false and the existing user, credential and workspace), a command identifier reused with different content is refused as an identifier conflict, an expired receipt is never executed as a new command, and an unknown outcome is retried only as the same command; the other plan statuses map to the service's typed refusals. The Identity service is registered by composition and resolves. No new table or migration is added; the read plans the production store needs (identity credential and recovery reads, workspace reads through a Workspace-owned read port) are added, and the CLOUD.11 plans are unchanged.

| Baseline (unreviewed unless accepted) | not-started Planning repair 2026-10-05: CLOUD.11 (complete, Cloud PR 54) ships the Identity core with no production IIdentityStore or IIdentityIdSource, so the registered service cannot be resolved; COM.16 (Cloud PR 55, in review) delivers the generic plan-execution port, which refuses any plan id outside the calling module's owner, so it cannot execute the shared family plan families.account-enrollment.create-user. |
| Notes | Planning repair 2026-10-05 ([DLV-34](../README.md#rule-dlv-34): a new task gives work no existing scope could hold; no obligation or acceptance is removed). Design #248 decision 4 left the binding of IIdentityStore to the plan-execution port with no owner, and CLOUD.11's record lists the identifier source and the receipt reused-identifier mapping with it; nothing in the graph owned any of the three. Decisions: (1) one new small task, not an extension of CLOUD.12: CLOUD.12 is an L task whose acceptance needs external provider accounts and DNS that this binding does not, so absorbing it would hold the offline store work behind the Operations Owner, and a separate task keeps the enrollment path testable offline on the oracle and gives CLOUD.13 and later Identity tasks the same base. (2) It starts after COM.16 (the plan-execution port, delivered when merged per [DLV-24](../README.md#rule-dlv-24)) and CLOUD.11, and only CLOUD.12 and CLOUD.13 start after it (CLOUD.12 is the first live-store consumer; CLOUD.13 runs the device-revocation family and the Device participant of enrollment through the family port); CLOUD.16 gains a COM.16 edge for its own token store; CLOUD.20 completes after it. GOV.16 needs only the static metadata CLOUD.11 delivered (its edge is on CLOUD.11 and is unchanged), and no in-scope task waits for this one. (3) The COM.16 port refuses a plan whose owner is not the caller, so a shared family plan cannot pass through it; the family-execution port is therefore a new generic port in Abstractions with its own adapter folder in Storage.D1, neither of which overlaps COM.16's folders, and every later family participant (CLOUD.13, CLOUD.39, CLOUD.42, CLOUD.53, CLOUD.63) reuses it. Until the Workspace module has a project, the enrollment initiator contributes the workspace statements by one explicit adapter exception. (4) Identifiers: random version 4 UUIDs from the platform's cryptographic generator (the Entitlement store uses the same form); a replayed enrollment discards the identifiers it generated for the retry. (5) No plan, table or migration changes, so no RES-cloud-storage-plans or migration append; if the replay mapping proves to need one, that is a planning repair. Pattern for every module (decided by the CLOUD.72 planning repair, 2026-10-05): a module project references only the Abstractions project, so each module task binds its own live store itself, under its own Persistence folder, as an internal class written against the Abstractions plan-execution port (IModulePlanPort, COM.16) and registered in the module's own Register entry with the port created for its own descriptor (IModulePlanPortFactory.For); a call to a shared transaction family goes through the Abstractions family port that CLOUD.72 adds. No module task may leave its store unbound while declaring a service that needs one. Planning repair 2026-10-08 ([DLV-34](../README.md#rule-dlv-34); [P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021)): the store, identifier-source, replay and family-port tests are C# tests in tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/Identity/** and Families/** with the same cases and negatives; no TypeScript store test is added, and the opt-in workerd run keeps its existing harness entry (eng/verification/d1-identity-local.ts). The reviewed 2026-10-05 workspace exception (the enrollment initiator contributes the workspace statements) stays as recorded in writes and is unchanged by this repair; no external prerequisite or new task is proposed here. No existing CLOUD.11 assertion is loosened. Outcome and evidence are otherwise unchanged. Planning repair fix8a 2026-10-10 ([DLV-34](../README.md#rule-dlv-34); coordinator ruling S54): the statement that no new plan is added conflicts with the merged code. The CLOUD.11 plans cannot back IIdentityStore: no plan reads workspace_workspace (the store's workspace reads by id and by owner, used by every service write and by enrollment sign-in), no plan reads identity_recovery_code (the active recovery-path read; a constant would change the typed refusal), and the credential plans identity.credential-find and identity.credential-list return no subject, no passkey material and no password_hash, so a complete credential record cannot be built; a store with partial records or constant reads would be a stub. Decisions: (1) three identity read plans (storage/plans/identity/credential-get.sql, credential-rows.sql and recovery-active.sql) and two workspace read plans (storage/plans/workspace/workspace-get.sql and workspace-by-owner.sql) are added, with the regenerated PlanManifest.g.cs and worker/storage/plans.generated.ts (RES-cloud-storage-plans, append) and the S29 Worker bundle digest and size pin in tests/worker/release-provenance.test.ts; the CLOUD.11 plans, the tables, the migrations and families.json are unchanged, so the outcome now says that no new table or migration is added. (2) The Identity store reads workspaces through a public primitives-only Workspace read port in the Abstractions project (namespace ArcForges.Cloud.Modules, per ModuleBoundaryTests), implemented in the Workspace module's own Persistence folder over that module's own plan port and bound by its Register entry, with its tests under tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/Workspace/** (the EntitlementGrantPort precedent: cross-module access goes through Abstractions and each module reads only its own tables; no ownership rule changes). The rejected alternative, letting the Identity plan port read the workspace plans by a second reviewed exception in ModuleBinding, would change COM.16's ownership rule and is not taken. (3) Exactly two IdentityError members, IdentifierConflict and ReceiptExpired, are added in Core/Domain/IdentityRules.cs; no rule or model changes, and every existing refusal keeps its meaning (S54(2)). (4) Production resolution (S54(3)): production binds no plan executor and no recovery generation (the gap COM.16 recorded). CLOUD.72 proves service resolution with a DI test in a composition that binds the plan executor (the foundation or test composition) and records production resolution as blocked on the production plan-executor composition (owner to be assigned by planning repair fix8), not proven; this repair does not narrow the outcome's statement that the service resolves. (5) Binding to the real Cloud layout at origin/main c291550f: the CLOUD.11 plan files, IdentityRules.cs, PlanManifest.g.cs, plans.generated.ts, release-provenance.test.ts, WorkspaceModule.cs and the Abstractions project exist; storage/plans/workspace, Abstractions/Workspace, Workspace/Persistence and tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/Workspace are new (the workspace owner is already registered in storage/plans/owners.json). The realm-level plan allowlist in tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/Reduction/StoragePlanGeneratorTests.cs gains rows only for the new read plans that carry no scope parameter, because its every-plan-is-scoped assertion would otherwise refuse them. (6) Correction of the reviewed text: the FamilyBinding item in writes ("no Workspace module project exists") and decision (3) of the 2026-10-05 note ("Until the Workspace module has a project") are inaccurate: src/ArcForges.Cloud.Modules.Workspace exists as an empty CLOUD.02 boundary (WorkspaceModule.cs with the workspace descriptor and no services). The reviewed enrollment exception (the enrollment initiator contributes the workspace statements of the account-enrollment family) is unchanged, because the Workspace module still has no store or write path; only its stated reason is corrected. Item (5) of the 2026-10-05 note (no RES-cloud-storage-plans append) is superseded by this repair, as that item anticipated. No obligation or acceptance is removed, and the edges are unchanged. Planning repair fix8 2026-10-10 (pointer; coordinator ruling S57(14); S54(3); [P2-026](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-026) item 20): the owner of the production plan-executor composition that S54(3) left to planning repair fix8 is CLOUD.87 (production business composition). This record's production-resolution limit therefore reads 'blocked on the production business composition (CLOUD.87), not proven'. CLOUD.87 is not a prerequisite edge of this task, and no acceptance, write or edge changes. |

<a id="task-cloud-85"></a>

### CLOUD.85 — Serve the Blazor WebAssembly Account and Chat profiles and the C# static Site from the proof origin

**Outcome.** Serves the Blazor WebAssembly Account and Chat production profiles and the C# static Site from the proof origin next to the Cloud API. The Cloud Worker's static-assets binding (env.proof only) serves the content-hashed Blazor profile bytes of WEB.40's web-profiles-<sha256>.tar release asset and the C# Site bytes of its web-site-<sha256>.tar release asset (a deterministic tar, D2 option A), after digest verification; /api, /session/v1 and /proof/v1 keep routing to the Worker first; the profiles are served under base href / (the framework stays at the root, and the shells at /account/index.html and /chat/index.html load it) with the exact CSP token set of each surface (App profiles: script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval' plus required hashes, never unsafe-eval or unsafe-inline, and style-src 'self'; the public Site keeps its strict set without wasm-unsafe-eval). The Worker stays a thin transport adapter: it makes no business decision and grants no access ([P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021) item 1). This is the serving path that [WP-06.05](../../work-packages/06-aot-jit-and-wasm-publish-proof.md#rule-wp-06.05) requires for PRF.11; CLOUD.71 remains complete for the React bytes.

<a id="task-cloud-84"></a>

### CLOUD.84 — Cloud TypeScript reduction: C# generated tables and policy, thin Worker adapters, proof code out of the production bundle

**Outcome.** The Cloud Worker (worker/**) contains only the thin Cloudflare platform adapter: Worker fetch entry and routing; ingress transport framing and size/time guards whose limits are generated from C#; Container, Durable Object, Queue and alarm lifecycle classes; D1 batch execution, R2, Workers AI, Vectorize and Static Assets binding facades; operator HMAC transport; and the @arcforges/ai-internal codec. The TypeScript credential, CSRF and Origin edge checks in worker/index.ts and worker/router.ts are kept only under the [P2-021](../../../decisions/phase-2-specification-decisions.md#rule-p2-021) edge-guard rule: C# remains authoritative and enforces the same rule, their parameters and allowlists are generated from the C# source, and the Worker only refuses early (a cost control) and never grants. The method/route table, admission constants, budgets and readiness vocabulary are generated from the C# host source into one generated TypeScript module (worker/generated/cloud-tables.ts) that a generate --check gate verifies; no hand-written copy remains and regex parsing of C# sources is removed. The decisions that move to C#, each with its destination: queue/coordination policy to the C# finite-job runner src/ArcForges.Cloud.Jobs (CLOUD.05); readiness evaluation beyond transport to src/ArcForges.Cloud/Readiness (CLOUD.08); storage-plan authority (plan authoring, the family grammar, the guard checks and the generation of PlanManifest.g.cs, storage/plans/families.expanded.json and worker/storage/plans.generated.ts) to src/ArcForges.Cloud.Storage.D1/Plans (CLOUD.02, CLOUD.04, CLOUD.06, CLOUD.11); correlation validation (worker/ingress/correlation.ts) to the existing src/ArcForges.Cloud/Ingress/Correlation.cs (CLOUD.69; the Worker only forwards correlation values); the D1 migration runner's lease, epoch fence, gating, sequence and receipt decisions to src/ArcForges.Cloud.Storage.D1/MigrationRunner (new), where the Node shim only invokes wrangler with the C#-supplied statements (brief section 5.7; eng/migrations/runner.ts, catalog.ts, sql.ts and clients.ts keep argument passing and wrangler calls only); and the deploy-time environment-to-database selection, release-plan reading and excluded-build and gate refusals (eng/migrations/deploy.ts) to the new folder src/ArcForges.Cloud.Storage.D1/Deploy. C# tests replace the TypeScript tests one-for-one with the same cases and limits in tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/Reduction (new), Generation (new) and Readiness (existing), and the existing tests/ArcForges.Cloud.Tests/IngressCorrelationTests.cs. The production bundle built from the Worker entry contains no proof-only route, fixture or verification code. The Cloud package.json stops depending on the retiring @arcforges/proto and @arcforges/api-client identities; @arcforges/ai-internal is the one npm Contracts dependency. The Kotlin consumer gate (tests/kotlin-consumer and tooling/kotlin.ts) is removed after AND.40 delivers. The D1 migration runner and deployment step remain Node only as the wrangler invocation shim.

<a id="task-cloud-86"></a>

### CLOUD.86 — WorkspaceService (list, get, settings with revision, health)

**Outcome.** The Workspace module serves its WorkspaceService methods List, Get, UpdateSettings and GetHealth in production code. The services live in the Workspace module, are written against the Workspace read port and the module's own plan port, and are reached by the host only through a primitives-only workspace service port in the Abstractions project. The host's WorkspaceService endpoint class (CLOUD.21) gains this task's partial-class file, which translates the generated messages to that port, and this task's rows in the endpoint registry and the operation-to-owner coverage table. List returns the caller's workspaces in the realm (at most one per owner, by the realm and owner unique index) in one page with no next cursor, and refuses a request cursor as invalid. Get returns one workspace with its settings only to its owner. UpdateSettings changes the name and the protection profile (the one admitted profile) under the expected revision read from the request's RequestMeta, through one guarded named plan that writes the owner receipt through the existing commit tail, so a stale revision is refused as conflict.revision_mismatch and commits nothing, a replay answers from its receipt, and the data region never changes. GetHealth returns the workspace id, state and the composed recovery generation; every health field whose owner has not delivered (lastBackupAt, storage quota usage and pending jobs) is left unset and recorded as 'blocked on <owner>, not proven', never fabricated. RequestDataDeletion, PreviewDataDeletion and GetDataDeletion stay unregistered and refused, with CLOUD.17 named as their closing task in the coverage table (S59(11)). No table or migration is added.

<a id="task-cloud-87"></a>

### CLOUD.87 — Production business composition

**Outcome.** The production Cloud host resolves the module services that today resolve only in the foundation proof or a test composition. A production composition module, listed in the host composition beside the COM.16 plan-port binding, binds IPlanExecutor to the signed storage.internal executor and supplies the realm's recovery generation from the production configuration, so the plan-port factory, the CLOUD.72 family port and every module store resolve in production; when any required value is missing or malformed it binds nothing, and readiness reports the missing binding instead of a partial success. The Entitlement service is not resolved by this task: EntitlementService also needs an IEntitlementDefinitionSource (an internal port of the Entitlement module), and its production binding is COM.06's (S59(8)), so this task claims production resolution of the Entitlement service only if COM.06 has merged first; otherwise that leg stays 'blocked on COM.06 (the IEntitlementDefinitionSource production binding), not proven'. The foundation proof routes stay disabled in production. The production Worker declares the business D1 database in the top-level d1_databases of wrangler.json, so the gated migration deployment applies the checked-in migrations to it instead of reporting not applicable, and the production CloudContainer registers storage.internal beside the hosts already registered on main (ai.internal, and CLOUD.12's egress-handler hosts if CLOUD.12 merged first) with enableInternet false. The Worker forwards to the production Container only the declared variable and secret names (the private-request signing keys, the realm id, the recovery generation, the provider credential names that CLOUD.12 declares and the query-cursor signing-key name that CLOUD.23 declares), never a value in source. The task deploys by the gated main push under RES-cloud-deployment. Provisioning the production database and the production secrets uses the existing CI Cloudflare environment where its credentials allow; otherwise each item is recorded as 'blocked on the Operations Owner provisioning <item>, not proven' with the exact commands, and the composition stays fail-closed. The proof environment, its entry and its bindings are unchanged.
